# Public Rust API inventory
#
# Maintained by hand against the public items in rust/src, grouped by module:
# there is no generator, so a change that adds or retires a public name edits
# this file too. Use it to check a name before writing it into the
# documentation.

  pub const ALL: [Self; 13] = [ Self::Utf8, Self::Utf16Le, Self::Utf16Be, Self::Ascii, Self::Latin1, Self::Latin2, Self::Latin9, Self::Cp1250, Self::Cp1251, Self::Cp1252, Self::Cp437, Self::Cp850, Self::MacRoman, ];

  pub fn byte_of(self, scalar: char) -> Option<u8>

  pub const fn decoder(self) -> Decoder
  pub const fn transcriber(self) -> Decoder  (the same carry, reading each run as `transcribe` does; never refuses a byte)

  impl IOBase, IOMedia for Transcoded<H>  (decoded bytes, with resume points behind every positional read)

### yggdryl::root::codec  [rust/src/codec.rs]
pub struct Level(u8);
  pub const NONE: Self = Self(0);
  pub const FAST: Self = Self(1);
  pub const DEFAULT: Self = Self(6);
  pub const BEST: Self = Self(9);
  pub const fn new(level: u8) -> Self
  pub const fn get(self) -> u8
  pub enum Codec
  pub const ALL: [Self; 5] = [ Self::Identity, Self::Gzip, Self::Zlib, Self::Deflate, Self::Zstd, ];
  pub const fn has_restarts(self) -> bool
  pub const fn restart_scan(self) -> RestartScan
pub struct RestartScan
  pub const fn consumed(&self) -> u64
  pub fn push(&mut self, chunk: &[u8], into: &mut Vec<u64>)
pub struct Restarts
  pub fn new(stride: u64, points: impl Into<std::sync::Arc<[u64]>>) -> Self
  pub const fn stride(&self) -> u64
  pub fn points(&self) -> &[u64]
  pub fn is_empty(&self) -> bool
  pub fn before(&self, position: u64) -> (u64, u64)
pub struct Encoder
  pub const fn codec(&self) -> Codec
  pub fn restart(&mut self) -> Result<()>
  pub fn finish(self) -> Result<()>
  pub fn from_str(value: &str) -> Result<Self>
  pub const fn as_str(self) -> &'static str
  pub const fn extension(self) -> Option<&'static str>
  pub const fn is_identity(self) -> bool
  pub fn from_mime_type(value: &MimeType) -> Self
  pub fn from_media_type(value: &MediaType) -> Self
  pub fn from_url(value: &Url) -> Self
  pub fn load(self, input: &[u8]) -> Result<Vec<u8>>
  pub fn dump(self, input: &[u8]) -> Result<Vec<u8>>
  pub fn dump_with_level(self, input: &[u8], level: Level) -> Result<Vec<u8>>
  pub fn reader<'source, R: Read + 'source>(self, source: R) -> Box<dyn Read + 'source>
  pub fn writer<'target, W: Write + 'target>(self, target: W) -> Encoder<'target>
  pub fn writer_with_level<'target, W: Write + 'target>( self, target: W, level: Level, ) -> Encoder<'target>
  pub fn finish(self) -> Result<()>

### yggdryl::variant  [rust/src/variant.rs]  (the Apache Parquet Variant binary encoding, version 1: one metadata dictionary and one value payload)
pub const VARIANT_VERSION: u8 = 1;  (the specification version, the low nibble of the metadata header)
pub const VARIANT_EXTENSION_NAME: &str = "arrow.parquet.variant";  (the canonical Arrow extension over the struct of two binaries)
pub const VARIANT_METADATA_FIELD: &str = "metadata";  (the child holding the key dictionary)
pub const VARIANT_VALUE_FIELD: &str = "value";  (the child holding the value payload)
pub struct Variant  (Clone, Eq/Ord/Hash, Debug, Display = the JSON the value spells; the value of Scalar::Variant and what a `variant` column stores per row)
  pub fn new(metadata: impl Into<Arc<[u8]>>, value: impl Into<Arc<[u8]>>) -> Result<Self>  (bytes a medium stored; the metadata header, its dictionary and its offsets are read here, the value payload by `scalar`)
  pub fn encode(value: &Scalar) -> Result<Self>  (the tree's object keys sorted into one dictionary, sizes the narrowest the payload allows; refuses what the standard cannot spell - a decimal past 38 digits, a clock that is not a whole microsecond, a zoned time or duration, a mapping keyed by anything but text)
  pub fn metadata(&self) -> &[u8]
  pub fn value(&self) -> &[u8]
  pub fn scalar(&self) -> Result<Scalar>  (the value the bytes hold, refused at the byte: a payload cut short, a primitive version 1 does not name, a field id past the dictionary, an object naming one key twice, bytes left over)

### yggdryl::valuestream  [rust/src/valuestream.rs]  (the crate's own byte stream: any Scalar, leaf for leaf, and back)
pub const VALUE_STREAM_VERSION: u8 = 0;  (the first byte of every stream)
pub const COMPRESS_FROM: usize = 4 * 1024;  (a payload of no fixed width past this many bytes is a zstd frame)
pub struct ValueStream  (Iterator<Item = Vec<u8>> + FusedIterator: one chunk per leaf, the children of a nested value after its header, holding what it has yet to write and nothing it wrote)

### yggdryl::root::datatype_id  [rust/src/datatype_id.rs]
pub enum DataTypeId
  pub const ALL: [Self; 84] = [all exact datatype identifiers in canonical declaration order, including FIGICode];  (every discriminant is explicit; values are partitioned by DataTypeKind rather than declaration position)
  pub fn from_str(value: &str) -> Result<Self>
  pub const fn as_str(self) -> &'static str
  pub const fn as_u8(self) -> u8  (one byte laid out by family: the family's own number first, `DataTypeKind::id`, then its leaves in the family's range; the tag the digest feed and the variant encoding write)
  pub const fn from_u8(byte: u8) -> Option<Self>  (the leaf one byte names; none for a family's own number, a placeholder in a family's range, or a byte past every family)
  pub const fn kind(self) -> DataTypeKind
  pub const fn is_parameterized(self) -> bool
  pub const fn is_integer(self) -> bool
  pub const fn is_signed_integer(self) -> bool
  pub const fn is_unsigned_integer(self) -> bool
  pub const fn is_floating(self) -> bool
  pub const fn is_decimal(self) -> bool
  pub const fn is_temporal(self) -> bool
  pub const fn is_binary(self) -> bool
  pub const fn is_string(self) -> bool  (the eighteen string leaves)
  pub const fn fixed_byte_width(self) -> Option<usize>  (the numbers, a UUID; a code answers code_width instead)
  pub const fn code_width(self) -> Option<usize>  (the twelve registered codes; the most bytes one value may be: cusip 9, sedol 7)

### yggdryl::root::datatype_kind  [rust/src/datatype_kind.rs]
pub enum DataTypeKind
  pub const ALL: [Self; 12] = [ Self::Null, Self::Boolean, Self::Integer, Self::Floating, Self::Decimal, Self::Temporal, Self::Text, Self::Code, Self::Bytes, Self::Nested, Self::Geospatial, Self::Uuid, ];
  pub fn from_str(value: &str) -> Result<Self>
  pub const fn as_str(self) -> &'static str
  pub const fn id(self) -> u8  (the family's own number, the start of the range its leaves take and a placeholder no leaf takes - but the null family's, whose one leaf is the null itself)
  pub const fn of_u8(byte: u8) -> Option<Self>  (the family whose range a byte is in, or none past every family)
  pub const fn is_nested(self) -> bool
  pub const fn is_wrapper(self) -> bool
  pub const fn is_numeric(self) -> bool
  pub const fn is_bytes(self) -> bool
  pub const fn is_ordered(self) -> bool

### yggdryl::fix::field  [rust/src/fix/field.rs]
impl<'field> FixField<'field>
  pub fn branches(&self) -> FixSpellings<'field>  (the dialects that contributed this field, folded and sorted; empty for a field the specification alone defines)
  pub fn has_branch(&self, dialect: &str) -> bool
  pub fn id(&self) -> Result<Option<FixId>>  (derived from `FIX:tag` and the field's name, never stored)
  pub fn tag(&self) -> Result<Option<i32>>  (positive decimal digits only)
  pub fn tags(&self) -> Result<Vec<i32>>  (the FIX:tags array, `[1088,152]`; refuses a stored text that is not the compact array the setter writes, a tag that is not positive, or one named twice)
  pub fn names(&self) -> Words<'field>  (the FIX:names array, `["Qty","Quantity"]`, walked lazily; a stored text that is not the array the setter writes yields nothing)
  pub fn identifiers(&self) -> FixSpellings<'field>  (canonical direct scalar members in component order; borrowed)
  pub fn nulls(&self) -> FixSpellings<'field>  (the FIX:nulls spellings that mean nothing was sent, lazy; nothing where absent)
  pub fn is_null_value(&self, value: &str) -> bool  (ASCII case-insensitive against the trimmed text)
  pub fn description(&self) -> Option<&'field str>
  pub fn component(&self) -> Option<&'field str>
  pub fn field_ref(&self) -> Option<&'field str>
  pub fn deprecated(&self) -> Option<&'field str>  (the FIX:deprecated version at which FIX removed this field; the dictionary keeps it so an old message resolves, and a parse restates it under what replaced it and nulls the source)
  pub fn group(&self) -> Option<&'field str>
  pub fn msgtype(&self) -> Option<&'field str>
  pub fn msgcat(&self) -> Option<&'field str>  (the fixed four-byte business category a message definition declares)
  pub fn counter(&self) -> Result<Option<i32>>  (positive decimal digits only)
  pub fn codeset(&self) -> Option<&'field str>  (the name of the FIX:codeset set this field's values are drawn from; the dictionary holds the members, and FixRegistry::codeset_of answers them)
  pub fn replacements(&self) -> FixReplacements<'field>
  pub fn directions(&self) -> FixDirections<'field>  (tag 385's FIX:directions rules in document order; nothing where absent, which reads by the defaults)
  pub fn is_transient(&self) -> Result<bool>  (the FIX:transient flag, true where absent: whether this column travels from one message of a chain to the next, so a column settled to one message says false)
  pub fn derivation(&self) -> Result<Option<Term>>  (the FIX:derivation term: how this field's value is derived from the message where the message states none, one expression over the message's fields by canonical folded name, parsed through Term::from_str and budget-checked; the complete generated 29-rule set takes the native evaluator while any edited, removed or added rule selects the generic evaluator for the whole registry; None where absent; InvalidMetadataValue naming FIX:derivation for a text that is not a term or one past the budget)
  impl FixFieldMut<'_>
  pub fn set_branches<I, S>(&mut self, dialects: I) -> Result<()> where I: IntoIterator<Item = S>, S: AsRef<str>,  (each non-empty and comma-free, lowercased, deduplicated under the fold, sorted)
  pub fn add_branch(&mut self, dialect: &str) -> Result<()>
  pub fn set_tag(&mut self, tag: i32) -> Result<()>  (any positive tag; zero is unresolved arrival provenance only)
  pub fn set_tags(&mut self, tags: &[i32]) -> Result<()>  (positive, distinct; stored as the compact JSON array; empty removes)
  pub fn set_names<I, S>(&mut self, names: I) -> Result<()> where I: IntoIterator<Item = S>, S: AsRef<str>,  (each non-empty and free of a quote, a backslash or a control character, distinct with ASCII case folded; stored as the compact JSON array; empty removes)
  pub fn set_identifiers<I, S>(&mut self, identifiers: I) -> Result<()> where I: IntoIterator<Item = S>, S: AsRef<str>  (resolve names, aliases or tags once against direct scalar members; atomic, empty removes)
  pub fn set_nulls<I, S>(&mut self, spellings: I) -> Result<()> where I: IntoIterator<Item = S>, S: AsRef<str>  (comma-separated; a spelling holding the separator or repeating another under ASCII case folding is refused; an empty spelling is admitted)
  pub fn set_description(&mut self, value: impl Into<String>) -> Result<()>
  pub fn set_component(&mut self, name: &str) -> Result<()>
  pub fn remove_component(&mut self) -> Option<String>
  pub fn set_field_ref(&mut self, name: &str) -> Result<()>
  pub fn remove_field_ref(&mut self) -> Option<String>
  pub fn set_deprecated(&mut self, version: Option<&str>) -> Result<()>  (None removes FIX:deprecated; an empty version is InvalidMetadataValue)
  pub fn set_group(&mut self, name: &str) -> Result<()>
  pub fn remove_group(&mut self) -> Option<String>
  pub fn set_msgtype(&mut self, name: &str) -> Result<()>
  pub fn remove_msgtype(&mut self) -> Option<String>
  pub fn set_counter(&mut self, tag: i32) -> Result<()>  (positive tag)
  pub fn remove_counter(&mut self) -> Result<Option<i32>>
  pub fn set_codeset(&mut self, name: &str) -> Result<()>  (names the set this field reads its values by; a name no store can file is InvalidRecord; empty removes; the members are stated with FixRegistry::set_codeset)
  pub fn remove_codeset(&mut self) -> Option<String>  (answers the name it held)
  pub fn set_replacements(&mut self, entries: &[FixReplacement]) -> Result<()>
  pub fn remove_replacements(&mut self) -> Result<Option<Vec<FixReplacement>>>
  pub fn set_directions(&mut self, directions: &[FixDirection]) -> Result<()>  (empty removes; refuses a code named twice under a folded spelling, an empty code, no pattern, an empty pattern, or a pattern regex::bytes refuses; the field is unchanged. Whether a code is one of the set is the dictionary's question: MsgDirection::from_registry resolves each against the set in force and drops one outside it with this setter's own refusal as the warning)
  pub fn remove_directions(&mut self) -> Result<Option<Vec<FixDirection>>>
  pub fn set_transient(&mut self, transient: bool) -> Result<()>  (writes FIX:transient; true is the default and is stored as stated)
  pub fn set_derivation(&mut self, term: &Term) -> Result<()>  (stores the term's canonical text; a term past the depth or node budget is Error::Parse and leaves the field unchanged; a registry validates it again at insert, update and load)
  pub fn remove_derivation(&mut self) -> Result<Option<Term>>  (answers the term it spelled; a stored text the reader refuses is InvalidMetadataValue, already removed)
  pub fn merge_with(&mut self, other: &FixField<'_>) -> Result<()>  (the same identity's metadata folded in under the per-key rules; a disagreeing tag is a typed conflict naming both sides, and any refusal leaves the field unchanged)
  pub struct FixSpellings<'field>  (the comma-separated properties: branches, identifiers and nulls)
  impl<'field> Iterator for FixSpellings<'field>  (Item = &'field str; also DoubleEndedIterator and FusedIterator)

### yggdryl::fix::codec  [rust/src/fix/codec.rs]
pub const SOH: u8 = 0x01;
pub const DEFAULT_NULL_VALUES: [&str; 6] = ["", "null", "<null>", "none", "n/a", "[n/a]"];
pub struct FixCodec
  pub const DEFAULT_BATCH_BYTE_SIZE: u64 = 128 * 1024 * 1024;  (the raw bytes one Arrow batch targets; a target, not a ceiling, and a batch always holds one row)
  pub const DEFAULT_BATCH_ROW_SIZE: usize = 32 * 1024;  (the rows one Arrow batch targets; a batch closes on whichever of the two bounds it reaches first)
  pub const DEFAULT_OFFICIAL_TIME_DELAY_MS: i64 = 1_000;  (how far from SendingTime(52) an official transaction clock may stand and still date the message, when the caller states none)
  pub fn new(registry: Arc<FixRegistry>) -> Self
  pub const fn registry(&self) -> &Arc<FixRegistry>
  pub const fn default_sending_time(&self) -> Option<&Scalar>
  pub fn set_default_sending_time(&mut self, value: Option<Scalar>) -> Result<()>
  pub fn try_with_default_sending_time(self, value: Option<Scalar>) -> Result<Self>  (exact ns/UTC or None; initial intake only)
  pub fn direction(&self) -> Option<&str>  (the code of tag 385's set an unmarked line takes on the batch door; the set's Send code by default)
  pub fn msgdirection(&self) -> &MsgDirection  (the registry's reading of tag 385, the field's FIX:directions or the defaults, every pattern compiled once when the codec is built)
  pub const fn batch_byte_size(&self) -> u64
  pub fn payload_column(&self) -> &str
  pub const fn separator(&self) -> Option<u8>
  pub fn null_values(&self) -> &[String]
  pub const fn with_separator(mut self, separator: u8) -> Self
  pub fn with_payload_column(mut self, column: impl Into<SmolStr>) -> Self
  pub fn with_capture_names<I, S>(mut self, names: I) -> Self where I: IntoIterator<Item = S>, S: AsRef<str>  (what a run's row-header captures are called, resolved once and read by position)
  pub fn try_with_direction(mut self, code: Option<&str>) -> Result<Self>  (any spelling of a code of tag 385's set, resolved once; None or empty = no pin; a spelling outside the set refused naming it)
  pub const fn with_batch_byte_size(mut self, bytes: u64) -> Self
  pub const fn with_batch_row_size(mut self, rows: usize) -> Self  (zero leaves the bytes to decide)
  pub const fn batch_row_size(&self) -> usize
  pub const fn with_threads(mut self, threads: usize) -> Self  (the threads the line and row doors read on: available CPUs by default; zero reads as one and one pulls lazily. With more, the line, message-row and write doors use PARALLEL_CHUNK items at lane depth two and answer in input order; parse_text_arrow_reader and parse_arrow_messages give whole input batches to at most this many jobs and yield them in batch order; lifecycle remains one ordered walk)
  pub const fn set_threads(&mut self, threads: usize)
  pub const fn threads(&self) -> usize
  pub const fn with_snapshot_ns(mut self, snapshot_ns: i64) -> Self  (the epoch-aligned lifecycle snapshot grid; zero or less disables it)
  pub const fn snapshot_ns(&self) -> Option<i64>  (the positive grid width, where enabled)
  pub const fn with_official_time_delay_ms(mut self, official_time_delay_ms: i64) -> Self  (how far from SendingTime(52) an official transaction clock may stand and still date the message: the parse takes the TransactTime(60) it states, else the TrdRegTimestamp(769) of the TrdRegTimestamps(768) group whose TrdRegTimestampType(770) stamps the event or a hop, nearest the sending clock; a nonpositive delay admits only a clock equal to it)
  pub const fn official_time_delay_ms(&self) -> i64  (the delay in milliseconds; DEFAULT_OFFICIAL_TIME_DELAY_MS when unstated)
  pub const PARALLEL_CHUNK: usize = 64  (the items in one lane chunk for the line, message-row and write doors; their lane depth is two)
  pub fn with_include_msgtypes<I, S>(mut self, spellings: I) -> Self where I: IntoIterator<Item = S>, S: AsRef<str>  (only these types are read; naming any clears the default refusals, since a caller saying what it wants has said what it does not; any spelling the dictionary resolves, a code it does not know kept as written, `unknown` naming a row that states no type)
  pub fn with_exclude_msgtypes<I, S>(mut self, spellings: I) -> Self where I: IntoIterator<Item = S>, S: AsRef<str>  (replaces DEFAULT_REFUSED_MSGTYPES; empty reads everything; a type named here is refused whether or not it is included)
  pub fn include_msgtypes(&self) -> &[SmolStr]
  pub fn exclude_msgtypes(&self) -> &[SmolStr]
  pub fn reads_msgtype(&self, spelling: &str) -> bool  (what the parse asks of the type a row states before it builds, and the walk of every message it is handed)
  pub fn with_null_values<I, S>(mut self, spellings: I) -> Self where I: IntoIterator<Item = S>, S: Into<String>
  pub fn infer_msgtype_bytes(line: &[u8]) -> Option<&[u8]>
  pub fn infer_msgtype_text(line: &str) -> Option<&str>
  pub fn parse_line(&self, row: &[u8]) -> Result<FixMessages>  (none, one or many messages a row: one per frame the row opens, one `unknown` message with no entries for a JSON document the row carries and no frame, and none at all where the row opens no frame, states no bridge pair and carries no document; every message built is parsed whole - typed facts lifted, a nested XmlData row exploded into it, deprecated fields restated to their latest aliases, the registry's FIX:derivation rules run through the native shipped plan or whole-registry generic custom fallback, identifiers and order lanes filled, the identity settled - and no later pass adds to it)
  pub fn parse_lines<I>(&self, lines: I) -> impl Iterator<Item = Result<FixMsg>> + use<I> where I: IntoIterator, I::Item: AsRef<[u8]>  (owns a codec clone; a line that is not a row is an `Err` item; fuses source exhaustion)
  pub fn parse_fix_line(&self, body: &[u8]) -> Result<FixMsg>  (one frame; a body holding a second is refused at the byte it opens at - `invalid fix expression at byte N: expected one frame, got a second`)
  pub fn parse_ullink_line(&self, body: &[u8]) -> Result<FixMsg>  (one bridge row; likewise refused, at the frame opening behind the row, target `ullink`)
  pub fn parse_fixml_line(&self, body: &[u8]) -> Result<FixMsg>  (one document; likewise refused, at a second root element, target `fixml`)
  pub fn parse_pairs<'a, I>(&self, pairs: I) -> Result<FixMsg> where I: IntoIterator<Item = (&'a [u8], &'a [u8])>  (one frame; likewise refused, at the second frame's key in the page the pairs were copied into)
  pub fn parse_text_line(&self, line: &TextLine) -> Result<FixMessages>  (takes the page the line already holds; copies nothing; states the line's `curruuid` as each message's one source - `srcuuids` - before it settles; none, one or many as `parse_line` answers, so a row opening no frame, stating no bridge pair and carrying no document yields nothing, and an empty payload nothing; malformed syntax may recover as one `unknown` message; mandatory clock/layout/content failures remain error items)
  pub fn parse_text_lines<I, L>(&self, lines: I) -> impl Iterator<Item = Result<FixMsg>> + use<I, L> where I: IntoIterator, I::Item: Into<Result<L>>, L: Borrow<TextLine>  (owned or borrowed lines and either Result; borrows without cloning, moves errors unchanged, fuses source exhaustion)
  pub fn lifecycle<I>(&self, messages: I) -> impl Iterator<Item = Result<FixMsg>> + use<I> where I: IntoIterator, I::Item: Into<Result<FixMsg>>  (the one walk, `graph::EventIterator`, over the messages: collected first; raw observations with the same complete nonempty capture `(msgtype, msgsessionid, msgctxid, msgseqnum)` ranked by greatest merge-reference recording clock then `currunix` - persisted `refrecdunix` where present, otherwise raw `recdunix` - and fully merged with the reference leading scalar conflicts, older facts filling absences, and repeating-group occurrences merged recursively at equal indexes in FIX tag order; `execunix` and `recdunix` retain their earliest duplicate-observation values and `refrecdunix` the latest selected reference; retained messages stably sorted by instant - a capture's lines are not in their messages' order - then each stated as the one after the live message of its chain, carrying the lifecycle's latest `execunix`; missing `parentorderid` takes a different predecessor `orderid`, missing `clordid` takes the predecessor's, and stated links win; the chain is keyed on the cross identity every incarnation shares with a fallback on a shared identifier; a message arriving under the identity the live one arrived under - the same message logged at another hop - restates it instead; errors move through in source order and never advance the walk; fused; a message whose sending clock the parse supplied is dated by its TransactTime(60) before the walk, `FixMsg::dated_by_transaction`)
  impl Clone for FixCodec
pub const DEFAULT_PAYLOAD_COLUMN: &str = "body";
pub const DEFAULT_REFUSED_MSGTYPES: [&str; 3] = ["0", "1", "unknown"];  (the types a codec refuses until a caller says otherwise: Heartbeat, TestRequest, and a row stating no type)

### yggdryl::fix::batch  [rust/src/fix/batch.rs]  (arrow feature)
impl FixCodec
  pub fn parse_text_arrow_reader(&self, source: BatchReader) -> Result<BatchReader>  (capture rows in, one FIX row per message out - a line carrying two frames is two rows and one carrying none is no row; the capture's columns lead and repeat over every message of a row, and the capture's own cells are read off the source row and carried by every message parsed out of it, stated again at their columns when the messages are written back; the row's `mtime` becomes each message's precise `recdunix`, with a raw observation's `refrecdunix` initially tracking it; a carrier's nineteen event columns are the line each row is: its `curruuid` is every message's one source, `srcuuids`, and the other eighteen fill nothing and are dropped as the row's own twins take their names; batches close as `arrow_reader` closes them, on the bytes each row lands as. With several threads, whole input batches are at most one job per thread in flight and answer in input-batch order)
  pub fn lifecycle_arrow_reader(&self, source: BatchReader) -> Result<BatchReader>  (FIX rows in, chained FIX rows out under the same schema: each row through `FixMsg::from_row`, the ordered walk `lifecycle` runs, each back into a row; no re-parse. Each row's capture cells are read into the message it makes and travel with it through the walk's own ordering, then are stated again at their own columns, so the pairing is by message and never by position)
  pub fn parse_arrow_messages(&self, source: BatchReader) -> Result<impl Iterator<Item = Result<FixMsg>> + Send + use<>>  (capture rows in, the messages the rows carry out: each row read cell by cell and parsed through the line funnel - the payload, the beginstring and msgdirection columns, the mtime column dating the row's line and becoming each message's `recdunix` with the raw reference clock tracking it, the carrier's `curruuid` column read as each message's one source where the carrier states one - else the identity the same bytes and instant derive - the other eighteen event columns filling nothing, every other column named after a dictionary field filling it - and the capture's own cells carried by every message parsed out of the row, under their columns; where each column sits is decided once from the schema; refuses a source with no payload column or one holding neither text nor bytes. With several threads, whole input batches are at most one job per thread in flight and answer in input-batch order)
  pub fn messages(&self, source: BatchReader) -> impl Iterator<Item = Result<FixMsg>> + Send + use<>  (each row through `FixMsg::from_row` under the source's schema, the capture's own cells read into the message with it, so a `messages` -> `arrow_reader` round trip over a carrying schema states them again at their columns; one thread holds one batch at a time, while several retain 64-row chunks at lane depth two that can span source batches, in source order)
  pub fn arrow_reader<I>(&self, schema: Field, messages: I) -> Result<BatchReader> where I: IntoIterator, I::Item: Into<Result<FixMsg>>, I::IntoIter: Send + 'static  (owned messages or results, each through `FixMsg::into_row` on the codec's threads in 64-item chunks at lane depth two; batches close on the bytes each row lands as - the leaves of every column it fills and a per-row width; completed prefix then error and fusion)
  pub fn format_messages<'codec, 'field, I>(&'codec self, messages: I, field: &'field Field) -> impl Iterator<Item = Result<Scalar>> + use<'codec, 'field, I> where I: IntoIterator, I::Item: Into<Result<FixMsg>>  (each message through `FixMsg::into_row` under one message field of the registry; a projected column is read from its holder or residual entry record; a packed occurrence, a composed key and a row-header capture are the codec's readings and are not in the record to lift)
  pub fn format_arrow_reader(&self, source: BatchReader, field: &Field) -> Result<BatchReader>  (the Arrow twin; the capture's own columns still lead the row, stated from the cells each message carries; a source carrying no residual entry record is cast through `arrow::cast_reader` instead of read back as messages)
  pub fn write_arrow_reader(&self, source: BatchReader, sink: impl std::io::Write) -> Result<u64>  (semantic content rebuilt from projected columns and the residual entry record, then written with the codec's separator else `SOH`; refuses a batch without `fixentries`)

pub const FIX_TYPED_TAGS: [i32; 29]  (re-exported at the crate root; every tag a message holds typed outside the crate's own block, in the order the wire states them - the header 8, 35, 49, 56, 34, 43, 52 and 385, the seventeen lifted 6, 11, 14, 17, 31, 32, 37, 38, 41, 44, 53, 117, 131, 151, 198, 262 and 1003, the trailer 93, 89 and 10, then Text(58); a caller walking a message's typed facts reads this rather than a list of its own, which drifts silently the moment a tag is lifted or retired. Every slot is an Option, and absent means the message never said: nothing writes here but a tag, so a slot's presence is the whole test of whether the fact reaches the wire, the residual entry record or the code the message digests to. ### yggdryl::fix::msg  [rust/src/fix/msg.rs]
pub struct FixMsg  (a market event with a FIX body around it: the `MarketEventData` the graph traits answer, the typed `FixHeader` carrying the frame, the typed `FixLifted` carrying the FIX numbers and identifiers the message lifted, the typed `FixCapture` holding the line's own bridge row header, `Text(58)`, a bridge's namespaced metadata, and the content row - every child the message states beyond those typed facts, typed by the registry's field for it; each fact held once. Every market fact the traits answer is *derived* from the lifted facts and the row, so it reaches neither the wire, the residual entry record nor the code the message digests to - only what the message stated does)
  impl From<FixMsg> for Result<FixMsg>  (Ok wrapper; permits infallible and fallible stream intake through one standard conversion)
  impl From<FixMsg> for MarketEventData  (moves the held generic event without re-reading or cloning FIX content)
  pub fn stable_hash(&self) -> u64
  pub fn new(field: Field, value: Scalar) -> Result<Self>  (links `FixRegistry::global()`)
  pub fn with_registry(registry: Arc<FixRegistry>, field: Field, value: Scalar) -> Result<Self>  (the value canonicalized under the root; a child stating a typed fact - one of `FIX_TYPED_TAGS` or a crate column - fills its holder and leaves the row; a child named under a namespace, `TECH.CLIENTID`, lands in the metadata; the clocks are settled and the identity derived; runs no derivation)
  pub const fn event(&self) -> &MarketEventData
  pub const fn header(&self) -> &FixHeader  (the standard header and trailer)
  pub const fn capture(&self) -> &FixCapture
  pub const fn lifted(&self) -> &FixLifted  (the FIX fields the message lifted, exactly as it stated them, including the message category; a fact the MarketElement getters answer but this holder lacks is derived, which is why it reaches neither the wire nor the code)
  pub fn text(&self) -> Option<&str>  (`Text(58)`)
  pub const fn metadata(&self) -> &BTreeMap<SmolStr, SmolStr>  (a bridge's namespaced keys, folded, in sorted order; empty where it stated none)
  pub fn entries(&self) -> &[FixEntry]  (the content row read as a tree, derived on the first ask and dropped by every write: one entry per non-null child, a group as one entry under its counter valued the count with one valueless entry per occurrence heading the members, a component as a valueless entry heading its members; the counter scalar beside a group is no entry of its own at any level, so a nested group states its count once; the typed facts are not entries)
  pub fn into_bytes(&self, separator: u8) -> Vec<u8>  (the message as it now stands: header tags 8, 35, 49, 56, 34, 43 and 52 where stated, then the lifted facts in tag order - 6, 11, 14, 17, 31, 32, 37, 38, 41, 44, 53, 117, 131, 151, 198, 262, 1003 - then the entries pre-order, then the trailer 93, 89, 10, which closes the frame whatever the body says. A coded fact spells as its wire code - `54=1`. A fact the message derived rather than stated is emitted nowhere)
  pub fn into_text(&self, separator: char) -> Result<String>  (InvalidRecord when a value holds a control byte)
  pub fn digest(&self) -> u128  (the XXH3-128 of what `into_bytes` emits in wire order, whatever separator either message was read with; what `FixDedup` keys on. `Element::get_currhashcode` is distinct: it frames and canonically sorts named sibling fields in the entry tree while preserving repeated occurrence order, so direct and reconstructed rows share one event identity.)
  pub const fn registry(&self) -> &Arc<FixRegistry>
  pub const fn as_field(&self) -> &Field  (the content row's root Struct: no typed fact in it)
  pub const fn as_value(&self) -> &Scalar  (the content row)
  pub fn get_by_id(&self, id: FixId) -> Option<Scalar>  (owned; a typed field's identity answers its holder, any other the child the identified field's name reaches)
  pub fn by_id(&self, id: FixId) -> Result<Scalar>
  pub fn get_by_tag(&self, tag: i32) -> Option<Scalar>  (a typed tag answers the holder's fact - `by_tag(35)` the type as text, `by_tag(52)` the sending clock as `datetime64(ns, UTC)`, `by_tag(54)` the side as `BUY`/`SELL`, a crate tag its column's type - but 65026, the capture's own column, which no message holds and which therefore answers nothing - `CURRHASHCODE_TAG_NAME` a UInt64, `CURRUUID_TAG_NAME` a Uuid - or nothing where the holder states none; any other tag the child carrying it, else the child named as the dictionary names the tag, else the child named by the tag's decimal)
  pub fn by_tag(&self, tag: i32) -> Result<Scalar>
  pub fn get_by_name(&self, name: &str) -> Option<Scalar>  (folds through the registry to the canonical spelling - a typed field answers its holder - then an exact root-child match)
  pub fn by_name(&self, name: &str) -> Result<Scalar>
  pub fn get_by_path(&self, path: &FieldPath) -> Option<Scalar>  (an indexed segment takes one occurrence: `Parties[0].PartyID`)
  pub fn by_path(&self, path: &FieldPath) -> Result<Scalar>
  pub fn get<'key>(&self, key: impl Into<FixKey<'key>>) -> Option<Scalar>  (a name that reaches nothing and spells more than one segment is read as a path)
  pub fn value<'key>(&self, key: impl Into<FixKey<'key>>) -> Result<Scalar>
  pub fn set<'key>(&mut self, key: impl Into<FixKey<'key>>, value: Scalar) -> Result<()>  (a key reaching a typed fact records it on its holder, a Null clearing it; any other key writes the row, typed by the field the key reaches, replaced in place or appended; a bare unknown tag appends a nullable utf8 child named by its decimal; a key reaching the capture's own column - sourceurl, by tag or by name - is refused, naming the column, because a message holds no fact for it and a row child would put it on the wire; a name nothing reaches is refused and the message unchanged; every write settles the identity again)
  pub fn set_many<'key, I, K>(&mut self, values: I) -> Result<()> where I: IntoIterator<Item = (K, Scalar)>, K: Into<FixKey<'key>>  (several `set`s with one rebuild; the later of two writes to one child lands; one refusal refuses them all)
  pub fn with_value<'key>(self, key: impl Into<FixKey<'key>>, value: Scalar) -> Result<Self>  (the consuming twin of `set`)
  pub fn remove<'key>(&mut self, key: impl Into<FixKey<'key>>) -> Result<Option<Scalar>>  (a typed fact cleared on its holder, a row child taken out, either answered; None for a key reaching nothing, a capture column among them - which `set` refuses rather than answering None)
  pub fn from_row(registry: Arc<FixRegistry>, schema: &Field, row: &Scalar) -> Result<Self>  (in schema.rs; the inverse of `into_row`: the typed facts read off their columns, content rebuilt from projected typed columns plus residual `fixentries` - each entry typed as the builder types a pair, a group no dictionary declares rebuilt as the list it is; every capture column is carried - the one the crate tags, sourceurl, and every column no tag and no counter names, each non-null cell under its column's name - and none reaches an entry, a digest or a `body=` on the wire; a column whose name holds a `.` is a bridge's own statement and lands in the metadata; `into_row` states each carried cell again at its column; a row without that column rebuilds its projected typed facts; `from_row` then `into_row` preserves the canonical row and semantic identity, not original arrival order or wire spelling; refuses a row the schema does not hold or an entries column that is not a residual entry)
  pub fn into_row(&self, schema: &Field) -> Result<Scalar>  (in schema.rs; each column filled by its tag or its group's counter, a column carrying neither by the child of its name; the capture's own columns answer the cell the message carries under the column's name, null where it carries none, because no message holds a fact for them, a value a nullable column will not hold as that column's null; tag 52 only where the message stated it; the residual entries close the row under `fixentries`)
  pub fn dated_by_transaction(self) -> Result<Self>  (the message dated by the TransactTime(60) it states with a clock where the parse supplied its sending clock - a carrier's, the default, the intake's - created at an earlier OrigSendingTime(122), its stand-in sending clock moved with it, and enriched again under the rules its date selects; a stated SendingTime stands, a day-only TransactTime dates nothing; what `lifecycle` reads before it walks)
  pub fn carried(&self) -> &[(SmolStr, Scalar)]  (the capture's own cells the message carries, each under the column it was read from: where the line was read from, its place in the object, the body it was cut from, when it was recorded; provenance and never content - none is an entry, none reaches the wire or the currhashcode - and `into_row` states each again at its column; empty for a message parsed from bytes, a row's for one parsed out of or read back from a row)
  pub fn set_carried(&mut self, cells: Vec<(SmolStr, Scalar)>)  (states the capture's own cells, replaced whole)
  impl Element, Event, MarketElement for FixMsg  (so MarketEvent; through the held `MarketEventData`: `is_after` is the instant, `finalize` settles the identity again, `with_previous` is `MarketEvent::following_market`, descending from the predecessor's whole lineage while the sources stay its own, `merge_with` is `MarketEvent::merging_market_event`)
  impl Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Hash for FixMsg  (equal over the holders, the row and the registry - the same Arc or equal registries; hashed over the currhashcode, the row's schema and its value)

impl FixRegistry
  pub fn get_msgtype(&self, spelling: &str) -> Option<&MsgType>  (one code namespace: the code's first holder answers a bare code; a name reaches the message of that name)
  pub fn msgtype(&self, spelling: &str) -> Result<&MsgType>
  pub fn get_codeset(&self, name: &str) -> Option<FixCodeSet<'_>>  (the code set held under the folded name; the lenient door beside codeset)
  pub fn codeset(&self, name: &str) -> Result<FixCodeSet<'_>>  (Error::Absent over "codesets" where none is held)
  pub fn codeset_of(&self, field: &Field) -> Option<FixCodeSet<'_>>  (the set a field's FIX:codeset names; the one door between a field and its vocabulary)
  pub fn codesets(&self) -> impl ExactSizeIterator<Item = FixCodeSet<'_>>  (every set held, in name order)
  pub fn set_codeset(&mut self, name: &str, codes: &[FixCode]) -> Result<()>  (states the members, replacing what the name held; rendered canonically in the caller's order; two codes sharing a name, or an empty value or name, is Error::Parse; a name no store can file is InvalidRecord; an empty slice removes the set and is a Conflict while a held field names it)
  pub fn merge_codeset(&mut self, name: &str, codes: &[FixCode]) -> Result<()>  (folds by wire value, keeping what the set already held: a placeholder name yields to a real one and every surviving spelling stays an alias)
  pub fn remove_codeset(&mut self, name: &str) -> Result<Option<Vec<FixCode>>>  (answers the members it held; a Conflict naming the first field that reads by it, because a field may not be left naming a vocabulary nothing states)

  pub fn definitions(&self, category: FixCategory) -> impl Iterator<Item = &Field>  (one category in the registry's own order, nothing collected; the field doors above reach a definition whatever category it is filed under, and these are the same definitions scoped to one)
  pub fn insert_definition(&mut self, category: FixCategory, field: Field) -> Result<Option<Field>>  (inserts or replaces, references validated before mutation, answering what it replaced; a Fields category is `insert`; a folded input name keeps the stored canonical spelling)
  pub fn create_definition(&mut self, category: FixCategory, field: Field) -> Result<()>  (refuses an existing name or wire identity atomically; creation reserves canonical identities only, so another field's alias may hold the spelling and a tag another field holds under another name is free - the identity is the pair)

### yggdryl::fix::registry  [rust/src/fix/registry.rs]
pub struct FixRegistry  (one namespace of scalar fields, components and groups, every one reached through the field doors below; a message is a component carrying FIX:msgtype)
  pub fn stable_hash(&self) -> u64
  pub fn msgdirection(&self) -> MsgDirection  (the reading of tag 385 as the registry holds it now - its set and its FIX:directions rules, the defaults where the field carries none)
  pub fn new() -> Self  (the crate's own definitions, then `SendingTime(52)` and `TransactTime(60)` seeded as nanosecond UTC clocks)
  pub fn from_fields<I>(fields: I) -> Result<Self> where I: IntoIterator<Item = Field>,
  pub fn get_field_by_id(&self, id: FixId) -> Option<&Field>  (a scalar by its identity, else a component or a group by the identity its derived tag and name make)
  pub fn field_by_id(&self, id: FixId) -> Result<&Field>
  pub fn get_field_by_tag(&self, tag: i32) -> Option<&Field>  (the tag's canonical holder, then an alternate; two fields may hold one tag under two names, and the first holder answers; a definition tag in `[DEFINITION_TAG_MIN, DEFINITION_TAG_MAX)` answers the component or group it identifies; a Map group's counter answers nothing here)
  pub fn field_by_tag(&self, tag: i32) -> Result<&Field>
  pub fn get_field_by_counter(&self, tag: i32) -> Option<&Field>  (the repeating group the counter `tag` opens - `453` the `Parties` List, `65020` the `identifiers` Map; two groups on one counter name nothing; the counter itself is `get_field_by_tag`'s)
  pub fn field_by_counter(&self, tag: i32) -> Result<&Field>  (absence or ambiguity raised)
  pub fn get_field_by_name(&self, name: &str) -> Option<&Field>  (a scalar by the canonical fold, then an alias fold; then a component, then a group of that name)
  pub fn field_by_name(&self, name: &str) -> Result<&Field>
  pub fn get_field_by_path(&self, path: &FieldPath) -> Option<&Field>  (a schema has no positions, so an indexed segment answers the item every occurrence holds)
  pub fn field_by_path(&self, path: &FieldPath) -> Result<&Field>
  pub fn get_field<'key>(&self, key: impl Into<FixKey<'key>>) -> Option<&Field>
  pub fn field<'key>(&self, key: impl Into<FixKey<'key>>) -> Result<&Field>
  pub fn contains<'key>(&self, key: impl Into<FixKey<'key>>) -> bool
  pub fn insert(&mut self, field: Field) -> Result<Option<Field>>  (a Struct inserts as a component, a List/LargeList of a Struct or a Map as a group, anything else as a scalar; replaces the same identity only, answering what it replaced; a held tag under another name adds the arrival beside the holder, which gains the arrival's name as an alias)
  pub fn update(&mut self, field: Field) -> Result<()>  (merges metadata for the existing identity under the per-key rules; a definition is replaced whole)
  pub fn add_field(&mut self, field: Field) -> Result<bool>  (true = arrived; a stored identity or a folded stored name/alias merges, a held tag under another name adds beside the holder, a nested field folds into the component or group of its name)
  pub fn add_fields<I>(&mut self, fields: I) -> Result<(usize, usize)> where I: IntoIterator<Item = Field>,  (added, merged) under add_field's rules, one staged mutation
  pub fn merge_with(&mut self, other: &Self) -> Result<(usize, usize)>  (code sets folded first, then scalars and definitions under add_field's rules, memberships unioned, one staged mutation; the counts are the scalars'. A name both dictionaries hold merges by wire value, and a field keeps the set it already reads by with the incoming set folded into it, so a merge widens a vocabulary and never narrows one)
  pub fn add_cfb_file(&mut self, handle: &dyn IOBase, dialect: Option<&str>) -> Result<(usize, usize)>  (the dialect or the handle's URL stem, merged under add_field's rules)
  pub fn add_cfb_files(&mut self, root: &dyn IOBase, pattern: &str, dialect: Option<&str>) -> Result<(usize, usize, usize)>  (files, added, merged) over IOBase::glob, private entries never matched, files folded in ascending URL order, the dialect or each file's own stem, one staged mutation for the whole call
  pub fn remove<'key>(&mut self, key: impl Into<FixKey<'key>>) -> Option<Field>  (a scalar, a component or a group; None when absent or still referenced)
  pub fn dialects(&self) -> Vec<String>  (every membership any field or definition names, sorted, each once)
  pub fn next_field_after(&self, after: Option<FixId>) -> Option<&Field>  (scalars only, tag-major, then id)
  pub fn iter(&self) -> FixFieldIter<'_>  (the scalars tag-major, the tag's holder first, then by identity; then the components and the groups in the catalog's name order)
  pub fn len(&self) -> usize  (scalars, components and groups)
  pub fn is_empty(&self) -> bool
  impl Clone, Default, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Hash for FixRegistry
  impl<'registry> IntoIterator for &'registry FixRegistry  (IntoIter = FixFieldIter<'registry>)
  pub struct FixFieldIter<'registry>
  impl<'registry> Iterator for FixFieldIter<'registry>  (Item = &'registry Field; also DoubleEndedIterator, ExactSizeIterator and FusedIterator)

### yggdryl::fix::schema  [rust/src/fix/schema.rs]
pub const HEADER_TAGS: [i32; 24]
pub const TRAILER_TAGS: [i32; 3]
pub const BODY_TAGS: [i32; 50]
pub const GROUP_TAGS: [i32; 3]
pub const FIXENTRIES_COLUMN: &str = "fixentries";  (the residual entry record group, counted by NOFIXENTRIES_TAG_NAME (65027); the one group the registry does not define, because a fixentry contains fixentries; one occurrence is tag/name/value/fixentries, three levels materialized and the rest folded into a JSON utf8 leaf)
pub fn fix_schema_tags() -> Vec<i32>  (the row in nine bands - when it happened, which event is observed, which message and session carried it, which instrument and what the market says about trading it, which order, what values it states as one ladder of prices and one of quantities before the two lanes, how it went, the four groups kept whole, then every standard header, body and trailer tag no band claimed, the metadata and the residual record's counter: 123 tags. Price(44), OrderQty(38) and Quantity(53) are among them, each exact and stated once; what a message is about is what `MarketElement::get_px` and `get_qty` read off them, and no column of this crate's restates either)
pub fn fix_schema(registry: &FixRegistry, name: impl Into<SmolStr>) -> Result<Field>  (the fixed row in `fix_schema_tags`'s band order: one column per tag the registry holds, named by the field's folded name, a List group beside its counter and a Map group on its own; 128 columns with the shipped dictionary; `beginstring`, `currunix`, `creaunix`, `currhashcode`, `crosshashcode`, `curruuid` and `crossuuid` required, every other column nullable; `cargo run --example fix_schema --features arrow` prints it as the Arrow batch schema, one column a line)
pub fn fix_schema_carrying(carrier: &Field, read: &Field) -> Result<Field>  (a capture's own columns in front of the fixed ones, less any whose folded name a FIX column takes; each carried column is made nullable whatever the capture declared, because only a pass holding the source row can state one)

impl FixRegistry
  pub fn from_json(input: &str) -> Result<Self>  (`{codesets, fields, components, groups}` and no other key; the code sets are read first, because a field naming one the dictionary does not hold is refused)
  pub fn into_json(&self) -> Result<String>
  pub fn add_json_file(&mut self, handle: &dyn IOBase) -> Result<(usize, usize)>  (added, merged) under merge_with's rules; no dialect - a snapshot states its own FIX:branches
  pub fn from_handle(handle: &dyn IOBase) -> Result<Self>  (`codesets/<name>.json` first - one document per named code set, stating its name and its members, refused when the name and the filename stem disagree - then `fields/<shard>.json` arrays under nine-digit shard names - tag 55 in `fields/000000000.json`, tag 5001 in `fields/000000050.json` - and one native Field document per component or group; a crate-tagged document and `components/fixmsg.json` are passed over, the crate's own definition being the one that types a row; references resolve once, missing names and cycles refused)
  pub fn write_into(&self, root: &mut dyn IOBase) -> Result<()>  (`commit` with its report discarded, one implementation so the two never answer differently)
  pub fn commit(&self, root: &mut dyn IOBase) -> Result<FixCommit>  (the three category directories and `codesets/`, and nothing else; writes the crate's own beside the dictionary's - `fields/000000650.json`, `groups/identifiers.json`, `groups/metadata.json` and the fixed row `components/fixmsg.json` - then removes every `.json` it did not write and every empty category directory. Each document is digested where it lies and skipped where it already states this registry, so a commit writes what moved and a second commit of one registry writes nothing)
pub struct FixCommit  (what one `commit` moved under a store root: `written` and `removed` name the documents, `skipped` counts the ones already stating the registry, because a store holds thousands a run leaves alone; `is_clean` is the replay answer and `len` what the commit considered)
  pub written: Vec<SmolStr>
  pub skipped: usize
  pub removed: Vec<SmolStr>
  pub fn is_clean(&self) -> bool
  pub fn len(&self) -> usize
  pub fn is_empty(&self) -> bool

pub const ULBRIDGE_ROWHEADER: &str  (the row header ULBridge writes in front of every line of its log: `timestamp`, `msgthreadid`, `msgsessionid`, `msgctxid`, `msgseqnum`, `msgpluginid`, `level`; `msgsessionid`, `msgctxid`, `msgseqnum` and `msgpluginid` are named for the fields they fill, and `timestamp`, `msgthreadid` and `level` name none and are the capture's own columns, so the header dates neither its line nor its message; kept ULBridge's because the line shape is the product's)

### yggdryl::fs::location  [rust/src/fs/location.rs]
pub struct BoundLocationIdentity  (one resource identity: filesystem equality domain plus exact raw path; its hash uses only the path, because Arrow exposes filesystem equality and no corresponding hash)
  impl Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Hash, Debug for BoundLocationIdentity
pub struct BoundLocation  (a filesystem and its exact path, with separate caller and diagnostic URIs)
  pub fn new(filesystem: Arc<dyn FileSystem>, path: impl Into<String>, uri: Option<String>) -> Result<Self>  (the injected path is neither parsed nor normalized, and `uri`, when supplied, is retained exactly and never used to derive the backend path)
  pub fn filesystem(&self) -> &Arc<dyn FileSystem>
  pub fn path(&self) -> &str
  pub fn uri(&self) -> Option<&str>
  pub fn masked_uri(&self) -> Option<&str>
  pub const fn diagnostic_url(&self) -> &Url
  pub fn identity(&self) -> BoundLocationIdentity
  pub fn same_location(&self, other: &Self) -> bool
  pub fn try_same_location(&self, other: &Self) -> Result<bool>  (fallible equality for host-runtime filesystem adapters)
  pub fn child(&self, name: &str) -> Result<Self>  (a raw child name, with no URL decoding or path normalization; the empty name and `.` both name this location)
  pub fn listed(&self, path: impl Into<String>) -> Result<Self>  (an exact path returned by this filesystem)
  pub fn parent(&self) -> Option<Result<Self>>  (the raw parent, preserving repeated separators and escapes)
  impl Clone, Debug, Display for BoundLocation  (both mask the path, and Display prefers the masked URI)
pub fn mask_uri(uri: &str) -> String  (mask URI user information and credential-like query values; re-exported at `yggdryl::fs`)

### yggdryl::fs::memory  [rust/src/fs/memory.rs]
pub struct MemoryFileSystem  (one isolated in-memory filesystem equality domain)
  pub fn new() -> Self
  impl Clone, Default, Debug, FileSystem for MemoryFileSystem

### yggdryl::fs::path  [rust/src/fs/path.rs]
pub struct FsPath  (a bound location whose current file/directory role is resolved per action)
  pub fn new(bound: BoundLocation) -> Self
  pub fn from_path(filesystem: std::sync::Arc<dyn FileSystem>, path: impl Into<String>, uri: Option<String>) -> Result<Self>
  pub const fn bound(&self) -> &BoundLocation
  pub fn filesystem(&self) -> &std::sync::Arc<dyn FileSystem>
  pub fn path(&self) -> &str
  pub const fn url(&self) -> &Url
  pub fn as_directory(&self) -> FsFolder  (without probing the location)
  pub fn as_file(&self) -> FsFile  (without probing the location; carries a declared media type across)

### yggdryl::fs::stream  [rust/src/fs/stream.rs]
pub trait ByteReader: Send  (a sequential input stream)
  fn read(&mut self, buffer: &mut [u8]) -> Result<usize>
  fn tell(&self) -> u64
  fn close(&mut self) -> Result<()>  (repeated calls after a successful close are successful)
  fn closed(&self) -> bool
  fn as_any(&self) -> &dyn Any
  fn into_any(self: Box<Self>) -> Box<dyn Any>
pub trait RandomAccessReader: ByteReader  (a seekable input file with positional reads)
  fn read_at(&mut self, offset: u64, buffer: &mut [u8]) -> Result<usize>  (without changing the current position)
  fn seek(&mut self, from: SeekFrom) -> Result<u64>
  fn into_random_any(self: Box<Self>) -> Box<dyn Any>
pub trait ByteWriter: Send  (a sequential output or append stream)
  fn write(&mut self, bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<usize>
  fn tell(&self) -> u64
  fn flush(&mut self) -> Result<()>  (publish buffered writes without closing)
  fn close(&mut self) -> Result<()>  (flush and close exactly once; a failed close remains visible)
  fn closed(&self) -> bool
  fn as_any(&self) -> &dyn Any
  fn into_any(self: Box<Self>) -> Box<dyn Any>

### yggdryl::fs::system  [rust/src/fs/system.rs]
pub struct FileSelector  (options for one directory listing)
  pub base_dir: String
  pub recursive: bool
  pub allow_not_found: bool
  pub fn new(base_dir: impl Into<String>, recursive: bool, allow_not_found: bool) -> Self
  impl Clone, Debug, Eq, Hash, Ord, PartialEq, PartialOrd for FileSelector
pub struct OutputMetadata(BTreeMap<String, String>)  (key/value metadata supplied while opening an output stream)
  pub fn from_entries(entries: impl IntoIterator<Item = (impl Into<String>, impl Into<String>)>) -> Self
  pub fn iter(&self) -> impl Iterator<Item = (&str, &str)>  (deterministic key order)
  pub fn is_empty(&self) -> bool
  impl Clone, Debug, Default, Eq, PartialEq for OutputMetadata
pub struct FileInfo  (what a filesystem reports about one exact path)
  pub path: String
  pub kind: IOKind
  pub size: Option<u64>
  pub mtime_ns: Option<i64>
  pub fn file(path: impl Into<String>, size: impl Into<Option<u64>>, mtime_ns: Option<i64>) -> Self
  pub fn directory(path: impl Into<String>, mtime_ns: Option<i64>) -> Self
  pub fn not_found(path: impl Into<String>) -> Self
  impl Clone, Debug, Eq, Hash, Ord, PartialEq, PartialOrd for FileInfo
pub struct FileInfos  (a deterministic listing that fuses after its first failure)
  pub fn empty() -> Self
  pub fn new(entries: impl Iterator<Item = Result<FileInfo>> + Send + Sync + 'static) -> Self
  pub fn failing(error: Error) -> Self
  impl Iterator for FileInfos  (Item = Result<FileInfo>; also FusedIterator)
  impl Debug, Default for FileInfos
pub trait FileSystem: Send + Sync + Any  (the complete synchronous Arrow filesystem contract)
  fn type_name(&self) -> &str  (backend name used only for safe diagnostics)
  fn equals(&self, other: &dyn FileSystem) -> bool  (including configuration and credential scope)
  fn try_equals(&self, other: &dyn FileSystem) -> Result<bool>  (defaults to `equals`; a language adapter overrides it so a handler exception is never reclassified as inequality)
  fn normalize_path(&self, path: &str) -> Result<String>
  fn file_info(&self, path: &str) -> Result<FileInfo>  (absence is a not-found FileInfo)
  fn list(&self, selector: &FileSelector) -> FileInfos  (ascending path order)
  fn create_dir(&self, path: &str, recursive: bool) -> Result<()>
  fn delete_dir(&self, path: &str) -> Result<()>  (the empty directory itself)
  fn delete_dir_recursive(&self, path: &str) -> Result<()>  (defaults to delete_dir_contents then delete_dir; overridden where the backend has one native recursive operation)
  fn delete_dir_contents(&self, path: &str, missing_dir_ok: bool) -> Result<()>  (the descendants, retaining the selected directory)
  fn delete_root_dir_contents(&self) -> Result<()>
  fn delete_file(&self, path: &str) -> Result<()>  (directories are rejected)
  fn copy_file(&self, source: &str, target: &str) -> Result<()>
  fn move_file(&self, source: &str, target: &str) -> Result<()>
  fn open_input_file(&self, path: &str) -> Result<Box<dyn RandomAccessReader>>
  fn open_input_stream(&self, path: &str) -> Result<Box<dyn ByteReader>>
  fn open_output_stream(&self, path: &str, metadata: Option<&OutputMetadata>) -> Result<Box<dyn ByteWriter>>  (truncating)
  fn open_append_stream(&self, path: &str, metadata: Option<&OutputMetadata>) -> Result<Box<dyn ByteWriter>>
  fn as_any(&self) -> &dyn Any  (the concrete backend, for a language binding)

### yggdryl::graph::element  [rust/src/graph/element.rs]
pub trait Element  (one element of a graph: its own identity, the identity it has in another graph and the code that names it there, the codes it digests to, the names it goes by, its parents' identities, its sources' identities and the order it stands in, all read and written; object-safe but for the order and the by-value readings)
  fn get_curruuid(&self) -> Uuid
  fn set_curruuid(&mut self, uuid: Uuid)
  fn get_crossuuid(&self) -> Uuid  (the cross element: what this element is in another graph; never absent - its own identity where it states no cross code, so every element stands in exactly one chain)
  fn set_crossuuid(&mut self, crossuuid: Uuid)
  fn get_crosscode(&self) -> &str  (the text naming the element in the other graph - an order's OrderID, a quote's QuoteID - the identifier every incarnation of one thing shares; empty where none is stated)
  fn set_crosscode(&mut self, crosscode: String)  (empty states none; `MarketEventData` eagerly brings the cross hash and cross identity in step and reprojects or retains-on-refusal `curruuid`, which rests on `currunix` and `currhashcode` alone and lands back where it stood, the cross hash no longer feeding it; its `set_crosshashcode` does the same projection directly; a foreign implementor owns whether an assigned identity survives its setters)
  fn get_currhashcode(&self) -> u64  (the XXH3-64 digest the element's content answers to, what `finalize` recomputes)
  fn set_currhashcode(&mut self, hashcode: u64)
  fn get_crosshashcode(&self) -> u64  (the XXH3-64 of the cross code; zero where none is stated)
  fn set_crosshashcode(&mut self, crosshashcode: u64)
  fn get_identifiers(&self) -> &BTreeMap<String, String>  (the names the element goes by elsewhere, each under the scheme that issued it, sorted; empty where no system named it)
  fn set_identifiers(&mut self, identifiers: BTreeMap<String, String>)  (replaces the map whole)
  fn get_parentuuids(&self) -> &[Uuid]  (the element's own ordered list; empty for a root)
  fn set_parentuuids(&mut self, parents: Vec<Uuid>)  (replaces the list whole; an empty one makes a root)
  fn get_srcuuids(&self) -> &[Uuid]  (the identities of the elements this one was read from - a message's text line; empty where it was read from no element; provenance, never lineage: no provided reading carries it along a chain)
  fn set_srcuuids(&mut self, sources: Vec<Uuid>)  (replaces the list whole)
  fn lineage(&self) -> Vec<Uuid>  (provided: its parents then itself, oldest first, each once - what an element following it descends from)
  fn is_after(&self, other: &Self) -> bool where Self: Sized  (the order the implementor states - an event's instant, a node's lineage; with `is_before` one strict weak order: never after itself, and neither-nor is equal)
  fn is_before(&self, other: &Self) -> bool where Self: Sized  (provided: `other.is_after(self)`)
  fn finalize(&mut self)  (the implementor's: brings the cross codes in step with `sync_cross`, digests its content from `digest` or the continuation its traits provide, and hands the code to `set_currhashcode` - or, for an event, to `Event::finalized`; `MarketEventData` always derives this way, while a foreign implementor may preserve an identity it owns by not calling the provided finalizer; every provided reading calls it once it changed the element)
  fn sync_cross(&mut self) -> bool  (provided: the cross hash code set to the cross code's XXH3-64, zero where none, and the cross element to `cross_uuid`; whether either moved)
  fn cross_uuid(&self) -> Uuid  (provided: RFC 9562 UUIDv8 over the cross hash code where it is not zero, else the element's own identity)
  fn digest(&self) -> Xxh3  (provided: an XXH3-64 state fed the cross code, the names it goes by and its parents, each under its own name, and never an identity, an instant or its sources; an implementor feeds its own content behind and reads `as_u64`)
  fn with_previous(self, previous: &Self) -> Option<Self> where Self: Sized  (this element stated as the one after `previous`, or nothing where it cannot follow it or following changes nothing; the implementor's, and an event delegates to `Event::following`)
  fn merge_with(self, other: &Self) -> Option<Self> where Self: Sized  (provided: another statement of the same element folded in - the cross code where this one states none, the cross codes brought in step, the identifiers this one lacks, the parents' union and the sources' union, each in this element's order then the other's - and finalized; nothing for another element or a fold that changes nothing; an event delegates to `Event::merging`, a market element to `MarketElement::merging_market`, a market event to `MarketEvent::merging_market_event`)
pub trait Event: Element  (an element that happened at one instant, stands in one state and sits at one place in its chain)
  fn get_currunix(&self) -> i64  (nanoseconds since the Unix epoch, UTC)
  fn set_currunix(&mut self, unix: i64)
  fn get_state(&self) -> &State  (the crate's ranked lifecycle code, never absent: `00UNKNOWN` where none was reached)
  fn set_state(&mut self, state: State)
  fn is_execution(&self) -> bool  (provided: whether this observation itself reports an execution, defaulting to `State::is_execution`; a protocol whose lifecycle state and report kind differ overrides it, as FIX does when an explicit correction or cancellation `ExecType` accompanies a filled `OrdStatus`)
  fn get_seqnum(&self) -> u64  (how many elements came before it in its chain)
  fn set_seqnum(&mut self, seqnum: u64)
  fn get_creaunix(&self) -> Option<i64>  (when it was created, in the same count, where known)
  fn set_creaunix(&mut self, unix: Option<i64>)
  fn get_execunix(&self) -> Option<i64>  (the latest precise execution instant this lifecycle has reached, in the same count, where known; an execution with no explicit value uses its own `currunix`, and a later non-execution carries the predecessor's value)
  fn set_execunix(&mut self, unix: Option<i64>)
  fn get_recdunix(&self) -> Option<i64>  (the precise recording instant, in the same count, where known)
  fn set_recdunix(&mut self, unix: Option<i64>)
  fn get_refrecdunix(&self) -> Option<i64>  (the recording clock of the observation selected as merge reference, where materialized; a raw observation falls back to `recdunix` during its first merge)
  fn set_refrecdunix(&mut self, unix: Option<i64>)
  fn get_exprtime(&self) -> Option<i64>  (when it expires, where it has an expiry)
  fn set_exprtime(&mut self, unix: Option<i64>)
  fn get_prevunix(&self) -> Option<i64>  (when the element it follows happened, where it follows one)
  fn set_prevunix(&mut self, unix: Option<i64>)
  fn get_prevuuid(&self) -> Option<Uuid>  (the identity of the element it follows, where it follows one)
  fn set_prevuuid(&mut self, uuid: Option<Uuid>)
  fn get_snapunix(&self) -> Option<i64>  (the opening instant of the grid step a walk read the element as the snapshot of, where one did)
  fn set_snapunix(&mut self, unix: Option<i64>)
  fn following(self, previous: &Self) -> Option<Self> where Self: Sized  (provided: fills an execution from its own `currunix` where needed, then keeps the later of that precise clock and the predecessor's latest execution clock; records the predecessor's identity and instant, the place after the predecessor's (saturating), forces the predecessor's cross code where this one's differs with the cross codes brought in step, takes the names the predecessor went by that this element lacks, descends from the predecessor's `lineage` - its parents then the predecessor, oldest first, each once - and folds the lifecycle; sources, recording and merge-reference recording instants stay this observation's own; nothing for its own predecessor, one that happened after it, or where nothing moves; finalized)
  fn restating(self, live: &Self) -> Self where Self: Sized  (provided, and overridden by every market event - `FixMsg` and `MarketEventData` - to take the market's place in the chain too: the step before it, `prevpx` and `prevqty`, and what the chain is about where this reading said nothing of it. This event as another statement of `live` - the same instant and content read a second time, one message logged at every hop - taking the place `live` holds in its chain: its predecessor, position and snapshot, the chain's cross code, the names and parents it knows - never its sources - the lifecycle folded, the earliest execution and public recording instants kept and the latest merge-reference recording clock persisted, then finalized, so both statements finalize to one identity and the chain grows by nothing; the caller establishes the twin by the identity `live` arrived under, which `EventIterator` records)
  fn merging(self, other: &Self) -> Option<Self> where Self: Sized  (provided: the element-level merge with the greatest merge-reference recording clock as reference - persisted `refrecdunix` where present, otherwise raw `recdunix`; stated recording beats absent, ties fall back to later `currunix`, an exact tie keeps this one - then that reference's instant, code, conflicting identifiers, list order, predecessor and snapshot leading; the further place in the chain, folded lifecycle, earliest execution and public recording instants, and latest `refrecdunix` retained; nothing for another element or a fold that changes nothing; finalized)
  fn fold_lifecycle(&mut self, other: &Self) -> bool where Self: Sized  (provided: the earliest creation, the latest expiration, the furthest state as `CodeValue::merge_with` reads two states; whether any moved)
  fn finalized(&mut self, hashcode: u64)  (provided: records the code, resets the current identity to `time_uuid` - kept where the instant has no UUIDv7 - and the cross element to `cross_uuid` over it; what an implementor's `Element::finalize` hands its content's digest to)
  fn digest_event(&self) -> Xxh3  (provided: `Element::digest` continued with the state, the place in the chain and the predecessor's identity; no instant)
  fn txhash(&self) -> Result<TxHash>  (provided: `currunix` at nanosecond resolution coupled with `currhashcode` as an XXH3-64 digest; ArithmeticOverflow past signed 64-bit nanoseconds)
  fn time_uuid(&self) -> Result<Uuid>  (provided: the UUIDv7 `TxHash::into_uuid()` answers for `txhash`: the microsecond instant is packed as the millisecond it falls in with the microsecond within it in rand_a's high ten, and `currhashcode` is stored whole behind it, so identities sort by microsecond and then by the whole code, and two events share one only where both agree; it passes neither the sequence nor the cross code because both are already inside that code - `Element::digest` feeds the cross code, the names and the parents, and `digest_event` the state, the place in the chain and the predecessor's identity - so rehashing them here would only spend bits restating them; what an implementor's `Element::get_curruuid` answers where its identity is its instant and its content)
pub trait MarketElement: Element  (an element that stands in a market: a price, a quantity, a side, two quote lanes, the instrument as the market names it and the market it traded on; no instant of its own)
  fn get_px(&self) -> Decimal18  (exact: `decimal128(38, 18)` preapplied)
  fn set_px(&mut self, px: Decimal18)
  fn get_currency(&self) -> &Currency
  fn set_currency(&mut self, currency: Currency)
  fn get_qty(&self) -> Decimal18
  fn set_qty(&mut self, qty: Decimal18)
  fn get_unit(&self) -> &str  (the unit the quantity is counted in, as the market spells it; empty where it says none)
  fn set_unit(&mut self, unit: String)
  fn get_side(&self) -> &Side  (FIX's Side(54), the crate's code)
  fn set_side(&mut self, side: Side)
  fn get_isincode(&self) -> Option<&IsinCode>
  fn set_isincode(&mut self, isincode: Option<IsinCode>)
  fn get_cusipcode(&self) -> Option<&CusipCode>
  fn set_cusipcode(&mut self, cusipcode: Option<CusipCode>)
  fn get_sedolcode(&self) -> Option<&SedolCode>
  fn set_sedolcode(&mut self, sedolcode: Option<SedolCode>)
  fn get_bloombergcode(&self) -> Option<&BloombergCode>
  fn set_bloombergcode(&mut self, bloombergcode: Option<BloombergCode>)
  fn get_figicode(&self) -> Option<&FIGICode>
  fn set_figicode(&mut self, figicode: Option<FIGICode>)
  fn get_cficode(&self) -> Option<&CfiCode>
  fn set_cficode(&mut self, cficode: Option<CfiCode>)
  fn get_miccode(&self) -> Option<&MicCode>  (the market it traded on, ISO 10383, where known)
  fn set_miccode(&mut self, miccode: Option<MicCode>)
  fn get_bidpx(&self) -> Option<Decimal18>  // and set_bidpx(Option<Decimal18>), get_bidcurrency() -> Option<&Currency> / set_bidcurrency(Option<Currency>), get_bidqty() -> Option<Decimal18> / set_bidqty, get_bidunit() -> Option<&str> / set_bidunit(Option<String>): the bid lane, what the element would pay; and get_askpx, get_askcurrency, get_askqty, get_askunit with their setters: the ask lane, what it would be paid
  fn get_lastpx(&self) -> Option<Decimal18>  // and set_lastpx(Option<Decimal18>), get_lastqty() / set_lastqty(Option<Decimal18>): what the element last traded, held under FIX's own LastPx(31) and LastQty(32)
  fn get_avgpx(&self) -> Option<Decimal18>  // and set_avgpx(Option<Decimal18>), get_cumqty() / set_cumqty, get_leavesqty() / set_leavesqty(Option<Decimal18>): what it averaged, how much is done and how much is left, under FIX's own AvgPx(6), CumQty(14) and LeavesQty(151)
  fn get_tif(&self) -> Option<&str>  // and set_tif(Option<String>): how long the element stands, FIX's TimeInForce(59) as the element states it - what the code `1` names is the dictionary's to say
  fn get_tradable(&self) -> Option<bool>  // and set_tradable(Option<bool>): whether the element can be traded right now, where the market says; `None` is a market that said nothing either way, which is not a `false`
  fn get_symbolticker(&self) -> Option<&str>  // and set_symbolticker(Option<String>): the ticker the instrument is known by, free text beside the codes rather than among them - the codes name it to a system, this names it to a person
  fn get_prevpx(&self) -> Option<Decimal18>  // and set_prevpx(Option<Decimal18>), get_prevqty() / set_prevqty(Option<Decimal18>): the price and quantity stated before this element - its own closing price where it carries one, else what the statement it follows settled on, which a walk fills
  fn fill_market(&mut self) where Self: Sized  (provided: every fact the element implies, read off the ones it states and nothing invented - the price is its own, else the last trade, else what it averaged, else its own side's lane; the quantity its own, else the last trade, else that lane's, with CumQty and LeavesQty deliberately off that ladder because together they are the quantity ordered and the dictionary already says so; the currency and the unit are its own, else that lane's; then `fill_lanes`. Idempotent, and what an implementor's `Element::finalize` runs before it digests)
  fn fill_lanes(&mut self) where Self: Sized  (provided: fills the lane the side implies - `Side::is_bid` the bid, `Side::is_ask` the ask, neither nothing - from the price, the currency, the quantity and the unit where the lane states nothing of its own; only a stated fact fills: a zero price or quantity, no currency, an empty unit fill nothing; what a lane states stands)
  fn digest_market(&self) -> Xxh3  (provided: `Element::digest` continued with the price, the currency, the quantity, the unit, the side, the last trade, the average, the progress, the time in force, whether it trades and the ticker where each is stated, each instrument code and the market named, and each lane fact stated; the previous step is left out, as the predecessor's instant and identity are)
  fn merging_market(self, other: &Self) -> Option<Self> where Self: Sized  (provided: the element-level merge, then this element's price, quantity, unit and lane facts standing, and the currency, the side and each instrument code the better of the two as `CodeValue::merge_with` reads them, this element leading; nothing for another element or a fold that changes nothing; finalized)
pub trait MarketEvent: Event + MarketElement  (blanket over every type that is both; nothing to implement)
  fn digest_market_event(&self) -> Xxh3  (provided: `Event::digest_event` continued with the market's facts as `digest_market` continues `digest`)
  fn following_market(self, previous: &Self) -> Option<Self> where Self: Sized  (provided: `Event::following`, then the price and the quantity that statement settled on as this event's `prevpx` and `prevqty` where it states none of its own, and what the chain is about where this event says nothing of it - the instrument's names and market, the currency, the unit, the side, the time in force, whether it trades, the ticker - this statement always leading; finalized where any moved. What `FixMsg::with_previous` and `MarketEventData::with_previous` delegate to)
  fn merging_market_event(self, other: &Self) -> Option<Self> where Self: Sized  (provided: `Event::merging`, then the merge-reference-recording-selected statement's price, quantity, unit and lane facts, and the currency, the side and each instrument code the better of the two, the reference leading and the other filling what it leaves unknown; equal reference recording and event instants keep this event leading; nothing where the fold changes nothing; finalized)
  impl<E: Event + MarketElement + ?Sized> MarketEvent for E
pub trait MarketEntryValue: MarketElement + From<MarketElementData> + Into<MarketElementData> + Sized  (a concrete market entry that moves through the canonical holder)
  fn into_entry<T: MarketEntryValue>(self) -> T  (moves every shared fact into another concrete entry wrapper)
  impl<T: MarketElement + From<MarketElementData> + Into<MarketElementData> + Sized> MarketEntryValue for T
pub trait MarketOperationValue: MarketEvent + From<MarketEventData> + Into<MarketEventData> + Sized  (a concrete market event that moves through the canonical holder)
  fn into_operation<T: MarketOperationValue>(self) -> T  (moves every shared fact into another concrete operation wrapper)
  impl<T: MarketEvent + From<MarketEventData> + Into<MarketEventData> + Sized> MarketOperationValue for T

### yggdryl::graph::column  [rust/src/graph/column.rs]
pub enum EventColumn { CurrUnix, CreaUnix, ExecUnix, RecdUnix, RefRecdUnix, ExprTime, PrevUnix, SnapUnix, CurrUuid, CrossUuid, CrossCode, CurrHashCode, CrossHashCode, PrevUuid, SeqNum, ParentUuids, SrcUuids, Identifiers, State }  (the nineteen columns every generated schema of an event states, one per fact `Element` and `Event` answer - a text line's batch in `ALL` order, a FIX row through the crate's protocol-oriented bands, the lifecycle's rows - one name and one datatype each, so the three join without a mapping)
  impl Clone, Copy, Debug, Eq, Hash, PartialEq for EventColumn

### yggdryl::graph::event  [rust/src/graph/event.rs]
pub struct MarketElementData  (every fact `Element` and `MarketElement` name held as one field each, no instant; `finalize` is `sync_cross`, then `digest_market` for the code, then the identity UUIDv8 over the code, so two elements stating the same things are one identity; its order is lineage - after the elements it descends from - and it follows another by descending from its whole `lineage`; a new one states nothing: a price and a quantity of nothing in no currency (`XXX`), no unit, a side of `UNKNOWN`, no instrument, no lane)
  impl Default, Clone, Debug, PartialEq for MarketElementData
  impl Element, MarketElement for MarketElementData
  impl<E: MarketElement + ?Sized> From<&E> for MarketElementData  (the facts any market element states, through the shared signatures; whatever else it states is left behind)
  impl From<MarketEventData> for MarketElementData  (the event without its instants)
pub struct MarketEventData  (every fact `Element`, `Event` and `MarketElement` name held as one field each, so a type that is a market event and more - a FixMsg - holds one and delegates; `finalize` digests through `digest_market_event` and hands the code to `finalized`; `is_after` is the instant, `with_previous` is `following_market`, `restating` is the market restatement and `merge_with` is `merging_market_event`)
  pub fn at(unix: i64) -> Self  (an event at `currunix` stating nothing else: a default element, a state of `00UNKNOWN`, no place in a chain, no creation, execution, recording, expiration, predecessor or snapshot)
  impl Default for MarketEventData  (at the epoch)
  impl Clone, Debug, PartialEq for MarketEventData
  impl Element, Event, MarketElement for MarketEventData  (so MarketEvent)
  impl<E: MarketEvent + ?Sized> From<&E> for MarketEventData
  impl From<MarketElementData> for MarketEventData  (the element at the epoch; `set_currunix` dates it and `finalize` then derives the identity)
